<!-- lem-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:derI}
There exists $(\varepsilon_0,\varepsilon_1,\cdots, \varepsilon_{n-1})$ that can be taken arbitrarily small and $C>0$,  such that:
\begin{equation}
\label{lya:lo:in}
    \frac{d}{dt}I +\rho \sum_{k=1}^{n-1}  \varepsilon_k |M_\omega N_\omega^{k} \widehat{V}|^2 \le C\varepsilon_0\max(\rho,\rho^3)| M_\omega  \widehat{V}|^2.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lem-1 -->
<!-- dfn-1: body_md -->
\label{dfn:SK}
Let $n\in \mathbb{N}\backslash\{0\}$, $N, M$  be $n\times n$ matrices with complex coefficients.
The pair $(N, M)$ is said to satisfy  the (\text{SK}) condition  if we have at the same time $M \phi =0 $ and $\lambda \phi+N \phi =0$ for some $\lambda\in \mathbb{R}$, if and only if $\phi=0_{\mathbb{R}^n}$.\\
 We say that the linear system \eqref{eq:lindiff} verifies Condition (SK) if the pair $(N_\omega, M_\omega)$ satisfies this condition for all $\omega \in  \mathbb{S}^{d-1}$.
<!-- end dfn-1 -->
<!-- lem-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:SK_diss}
    Let $N$ and $M$ be two $n\times n$ complex-valued matrices.                                                                                                                                  
                     
                           
                                                                                                                                                 
                       
        Then, the following properties are equivalent:
        \begin{enumerate}
            \item For all $\varepsilon_0,\dots \varepsilon_{n-1}$, we have $\sum_{l=0}^{n-1}\varepsilon_l|MN^l\eta|^2>0$ for all $\eta\in  \mathbb{S}^{n-1}$.
            \item $(N, M)$ satisfies the Kalman rank condition: the rank of the $n^2\times n$ matrix $
            \begin{pmatrix}
                &M\\
                &MN\\
                &\dots\\
                &MN^{n-1}
            \end{pmatrix}
            $
            is equal to $n$.
            \item $(N, M)$ satisfies the (SK) condition: the intersection between ${\rm ker} M$ and the linear space of all eigenvectors of $N $ is reduced to $\{0\}$.
                                                                     
        \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-2 -->
<!-- rmq-1: body_md -->
\label{rmq:cnd:Z:B:H}
A few remarks are in order:
\begin{itemize}
   
   \item Assume that $S^0_{11}$ is the identity.  Then, Condition (\textbf{H4})  is reduced to 
   the fact that   the matrices
$ S^\alpha_{11}(U)$  commute, in accordance with the work by  Ph. Brenner in \cite{Bren66}.
 \item 
 As pointed out in \cite{BaratDan23} (see also Lemma \ref{lem:equi:SK:positi:V1}), 
 assuming the matrix $ S_{12}(\omega)Z(\omega)^{-1}S_{21}(\omega) $ to be positive is equivalent to the (SK) condition when $ \overline{S}^0$ is identity, $P$ is an orthogonal matrix and $ \overline{S}^\alpha_{11}=0$, for all $\alpha=1,\cdots, d$. 
 \item 
 Assume that the matrices $\overline{S}^{0}_{11}$, $S^\alpha_{11}(U)$ ($\alpha=1,\cdots, d$) and $ S_{12}(\xi)Z(\xi)^{-1}S_{21}(\xi) $ 
 commute. This implies assumption (\textbf{H4}) and that $P$ is an orthogonal matrix. If we additionally assume (\textbf{H5}), then assumption (\textbf{H6}) is met provided that the rank of the matrix $S_{21}(\xi)$ equals $n_1$  and $\xi\mapsto   S_{12}(\xi)Z(\xi)^{-1}S_{21}(\xi) $ is a constant matrix.

In particular, when $n_1=1$, the
 assumptions (\textbf{H4}),  (\textbf{H5}) and (\textbf{H6}) are satisfied if the matrix $S_{21}(\xi)\neq 0$
and $\xi\mapsto   S_{12}(\xi)Z(\xi)^{-1}S_{21}(\xi) $ is constant. These conditions are, for example, satisfied by the compressible Navier-Stokes system (see Section \ref{sec:NSF}).
   \end{itemize}
<!-- end rmq-1 -->
<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:p}
    Let $d\ge 2$, $ 2\le  p_1\le p_2< 2d $ and $p_2\le \min( 4,\frac{2d}{d-2})$.  Let the (SK) condition for the pairs ($N_\omega,M_\omega$)$_{\omega \in \mathbb{S}^{d-1}}$, defined in \eqref{A:def}-\eqref{B_ome:def} and the hypotheses (\textbf{H1})-(\textbf{H7}) be in force.
        There exists an integer $N_0$ (depending on the matrices involved in system \eqref{systemE}) and a positive constant $c_0$ such that for any data $V_0=(V_0^1,V_0^2)$ satisfying
    $V^1_0\in \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_1}+1}_{p_1,1}$ and $ V^2_0\in \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_1}}_{p_1,1},$
    if, in addition,    
                                                                                                                                                         
    \[   \left\Vert {V_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}_{2,1}}^{l,N_0} +\left\Vert {V^1_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}^{h,N_0}+ \left\Vert {V^2_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}^{h,N_0} \le c_0, \]
    then \eqref{eq:base:V} has a unique global-in-time solution in the space 
    $\mathbb{E}_{p_1}\cap \mathbb{E}_{p_2}$ with $\mathbb{E}_{p_i}$ defined by
    \begin{align}
        \label{def:E}
        \begin{split}
            V^{l,N_0} \in & \mathcal{C}_b(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}_{2,1})\cap L^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}+1}_{2,1}); \;\;  V^{1,H,N_0}\in \mathcal{C}_b(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})\cap L^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}) \\
            & V^{2,H,N_0}, W^{H,N_0}\in \mathcal{C}_b(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})\cap L^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1});\;\; \partial_t V\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}) 
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
with $W$ defined in \eqref{W:def:2}.
                                                                                          
\medbreak
Moreover, we have the following bound for $1\le i\le 2$:
\begin{align*}
    \mathbb{V}_{p_i}&\lesssim  \mathbb{V}_{{p_i},0}\overset{\rm def}{=} \left\Vert {V_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}_{2,1}}^{l,N_0} +\left\Vert {V^1_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}^h+ \left\Vert {V^2_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}^h
                                                                                                                                                                                 
\end{align*}
with
\begin{multline*}
      \mathbb{V}_{p_i}\overset{\rm def}{=} \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}_{2,1})}^{l,N_0}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}+1}_{2,1})}^{l,N_0} + \Vert V^1\Vert_{L^\infty\cap L^1(\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})}^{h,N_0}\\
    + 
    \left\Vert {(W,V^2)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})}^{h,N_0} + \left\Vert {(W,V^2)} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1})}^{h,N_0}
    +\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})}.
\end{multline*}
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- rmq-2: body_md -->
\label{rmq:srong:cnd:p}
    This theorem improves our result obtained in \cite{DanADOglob}, as
here one may take the high frequencies of the data belonging to Besov spaces related to $L^p$ (with $p\ge 2$), which are larger. However, our structural assumptions (\textbf{H1})-(\textbf{H7}) are stronger than those in \cite{DanADOglob}. As in \cite{BaratDan23}, we have some restrictions on $p_1$ and $p_2$, that
is, $ 2\le p_1\le p_2\le \max(2d,2d/(d-2))$. This is due to the interactions between low and high frequencies
in the nonlinear terms. Let us also underline that we need smallness only in space related to $L^{p_2}$, so $V_0$ has to be taken so that $\left\Vert {V^1_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_1}+1}_{p_1,1}}^{h,N_0}+ \left\Vert {V^2_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_1}}_{p_1,1}}^{h,N_0}$ is large.
<!-- end rmq-2 -->
<!-- rmq-3: body_md -->
\label{uniq:p2}
    Uniqueness holds in a space larger than $\mathbb{E}_{p_i}$: as we will see in Section \ref{sec:uni:p}, uniqueness holds true in the space $\mathbb{F}_T$ of functions $(V^1,V^2)$ such that $V^1\in \mathcal C([0,T];\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})$ and $ V^2\in \mathcal C([0,T];\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1})\cap L^1_T(\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})$ with $\left\Vert {V^1} \right\Vert_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}_T(\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\le c$ (for some positive small constant $c$ depending only on $d$, $p_2$ and on the matrices of System \eqref{eq:base:V}) and $2\le p_2<2d$.
<!-- end rmq-3 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:p:s}
    Let $d\ge 2$ and assume that the Condition (SK) for the pairs ($N_\omega,M_\omega$)$_{\omega \in \mathbb{S}^{d-1}}$, defined in \eqref{A:def}-\eqref{B_ome:def},  and the hypotheses  (\textbf{H1})-(\textbf{H7})  are satisfied. Assume that $V^1_0\in \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_1}+1}_{p_1,1}$, $ V^2_0\in \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_1}}_{p_1,1} $ for some $p_1\ge 2$. Let $p_2\ge p_1$. Then there exists an integer $N_0$ such that if we make the following additional  (lower order) assumptions 
    \[  V^2, V^{1,l,N_0}\in \dot{B}^{s}_{2,r} ;\;\; V^{1,h,N_0}\in   \dot{B}^{s+1}_{2,r} \]
    for some $r\in [1,\infty]$ and $s$ such that
    \begin{align}
        \label{def:s:r:p2}
       -\min(1,\frac{d}{p_2})<  s<\frac{d}{2}-1 \;\; \text{and}\;\; r>1 \quad \text{or} \quad s=\frac{d}{2}-1 \;\; \text{and}\;\; r=1.
    \end{align}
    then there exist some positive constants $c$ and $C$ such that if 
   \begin{align*}
       \left\Vert {(V^2_0,V^{1,l,N_0}_0)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,r}}+  \left\Vert {V^{1,h,N_0}_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{s+1}_{2,r}} +\left\Vert {V^{2,h,N_0}_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V^{1,h,N_0}_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\le  c
   \end{align*}
     then \eqref{eq:base:V} has  a global-in-time solution in the space
     $\mathfrak{E}_{p_1}^s\cap \mathfrak{E}_{p_2}^s$ with   $\mathfrak{E}_{p_i}^s$  defined by
    \begin{align}
        \label{def:E:s}
        \begin{split}
          &  V^2, V^{1,l,N_0}\in \widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_b(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{s}_{2,r})\cap \widetilde{L}^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{s+2}_{2,r}); \;\;  V^{1,h,N_0}\in \widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_b(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{s+1}_{2,r})\cap \widetilde{L}^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{s+1}_{2,r})\\
            &  V^{1,H,N_0}\in \mathcal{C}_b(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})\cap L^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}) ;\;\; \partial_t V\in L^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})  \\
            & V^{2,H,N_0}, W^{H,N_0}\in \mathcal{C}_b(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})\cap L^1(\mathbb{R}^+,\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}), 
        \end{split}
    \end{align}
with $W$ defined in \eqref{W:def:2}.
                                                                                          
\medbreak
Moreover, we have the following bound for all $t\ge 0$ and $i=1,2$:
\begin{align*}
   X_{p_i}(t)\lesssim  \left\Vert {(V^2_0,V^{1,l,N_0}_0)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{s}_{2,r}}+  \left\Vert {V^{1,h,N_0}_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{s+1}_{2,r}} +\left\Vert {V^{2,h,N_0}_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}+ \left\Vert {V^{1,h,N_0}_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}
\end{align*}
with 
\begin{multline*}
 X_{p_i}(t) \overset{\rm def}{=} \left\Vert {(V^2,V^{1,l,N_0})} \right\Vert_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}_t(\dot{B}^{s}_{2,r})}+\left\Vert {(V^2,V^{1,l,N_0})} \right\Vert_{\widetilde{L}^{1}_t(\dot{B}^{s+2}_{2,r})}+\left\Vert {V^{1,h,N_0}} \right\Vert_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}_t(\dot{B}^{s+1}_{2,r})}\\+\left\Vert {V^{1,h,N_0}} \right\Vert_{\widetilde{L}^{1}_t(\dot{B}^{s+1}_{2,r})}
    + \Vert V^1\Vert_{L^\infty_t\cap L^1_t(\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})}+ 
    \left\Vert {(W,V^2)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_t(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})}^{h,N_0} + \left\Vert {(W,V^2)} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_t(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1})}^{h,N_0}+\\
    \left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_t(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})}.
\end{multline*}

In addition, uniqueness holds for $p_2<2d$.
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- rmq-4: body_md -->
\label{rmq:s}
    Let us notice that in our result, contrary with the results in \cite{DanCha10,Haspot11,ChenMia10}, we don't have any restriction on $p_i$ in the existence part. We can take $p_2$ and then  $p_1$ as large as we want. This is due to the fact
    that here we ask one more degree of regularity for the high frequencies part of $V$.
<!-- end rmq-4 -->
<!-- rmq-5: body_md -->
If $r=\infty$, then in the above theorem we must replace the strong continuity in $\dot{B}^{s}_{2,r}$ by the weak continuity.
<!-- end rmq-5 -->
<!-- lem-3: body_md -->
\label{lem_esti_partial_t_V_2}
     Let hypotheses (\textbf{H1})-(\textbf{H7}) be in force, and \eqref{smaal:cond:V} be satisfied.
     \begin{enumerate}
         \item Let $q\in [2,+\infty] $ and $\kappa\in [\frac{d}{q}-1,\frac{d}{q}]$.  There exists a constant $C=C_{\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}}}>0$ 
	  such that 
\begin{equation} \label{pt:V:est}
        \left\Vert { \partial_t V } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}} \le C(\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}} +1)\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}}+
      C\left(\left\Vert {V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+2}_{q,1}}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+1}_{q,1}} \left\Vert {V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}+1}_{q,1}}\right)\cdotp
\end{equation}
    
    \item Let $2\le p_1\le p_2$. There exists a constant $C>0$ depending only on $p_1,p_2, d$ and the matrices involved in the system \eqref{systemE}, such that 

\begin{multline}
     \label{pt:V:est:p1}
    \left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_1}}}_{p_1,1}}\le C\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_1}}}_{p_1,1}}+ C\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_1}}}_{p_1,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}+C\left\Vert {\nabla^2 V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_1}}}_{p_1,1}}\\
   + C(1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_1}}}_{p_1,1}})\biggl(\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}^2+\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}+\left\Vert {\nabla^2 V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}\biggr)\cdotp
\end{multline}
 \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-3 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
The proof relies on the product estimates of Proposition \ref{propo_produc_BH} and on the composition estimate of Proposition \ref{propo:comp}. We recall  from\eqref{eq:base:V} that
\begin{equation}
	\label{sysdif::modif}
	 \partial_t V = (S^0(U))^{-1}f-\sum_{\alpha}{(S^0(U))^{-1}S^\alpha (U) \partial_\alpha U}  +\sum_{\alpha,\beta}{ (S^0(U))^{-1}\partial_\alpha (Y(U)^{\alpha\beta} \partial_\beta V)  }.
	\end{equation}
 So, as $-d/p <\kappa \le d/p $, using product estimates \eqref{product_propo2} combined with Proposition  \ref{propo:comp}  yields (keep in mind the structure of $f$ defined in (\textbf{H7})): 
 \begin{equation*}
  \left\Vert {  (S^0(U))^{-1}f } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}}\lesssim \Bigl( 1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}}\Bigr) \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}} \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}},\end{equation*}
  and thus 
  \begin{multline*}
     \left\Vert { \partial_t V } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}} \lesssim \Bigl( 1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}}\Bigr) \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}} \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}}+ \Bigl( 1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}}\Bigr) \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}}\\
    +     \Bigl( 1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}}\Bigr) \sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^d \left\Vert {\partial_\alpha((Y(u)^{\alpha\beta} \partial_\beta V)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}}.
 \end{multline*}
 Hence, using \eqref{product_propo3:chap2} for the last term, and remembering the form of each $ Y^{\alpha\beta} $ yields 
 \begin{align*}
     \left\Vert { \partial_t V } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}} \lesssim  \Bigl( 1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}}\Bigr) \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}} \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}}+\Bigl( 1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}}\Bigr) \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{q,1}}\\
     +
     ( 1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}})  \Bigl(( 1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}}_{q,1}}) \left\Vert {V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+2}_{q,1}}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+1}_{q,1}} \left\Vert {V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{q}+1}_{q,1}}\Bigr)\cdotp
 \end{align*}

 Regarding the proof of Inequality \eqref{pt:V:est:p1}, it suffices to adapt the previous proof by utilizing the smallness condition \eqref{smaal:cond:V} and the product estimate \eqref{product_propo:p1:p2} instead of \eqref{product_propo3:chap2}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- propo-1: body_md -->
\label{propo:est:hf}
    Assume that $2\le p_1\le p_2$ and let $V$ be a smooth solution of \eqref{eq:base:V} under Assumptions (\textbf{H1})-(\textbf{H7}). Then,

     \begin{equation}
        \label{est:V:W:hf}
         \left\Vert {(W,V^2)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})}^h+ \left\Vert {(W,V^2)} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1})}^h+\Vert V^1\Vert_{(L^\infty\cap L^1)(\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})}^h\lesssim  \mathbb{V}_{{p_i},0}+(2^{-N_{0}}+ \mathbb{V}_{p_2}+\mathbb{V}_{p_2}^2)\mathbb{V}_{p_i},
    \end{equation}
    provided $N_{0}$ is sufficiently  large.
<!-- end propo-1 -->
<!-- lem-4: body_md -->
\label{est:M:lem}
It holds that
  \begin{multline}\label{2:est:p:W}
  \left\Vert {W} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1})}^h+ \left\Vert {W} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1})}^h \lesssim  \left\Vert {W_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_1,1}}^h+\int^T_0\biggl( 2^{-N_{0}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}^h+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}^2\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\\
+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i},\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2},\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}
    + \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}}\biggr)
\end{multline}   
where hereafter $ \dot{B}^{s,s^{\prime}}_{p,r}\overset{\rm def}{=} \dot{B}^{s}_{p,r}\cap \dot{B}^{s^{\prime}}_{p,r}$ and $\lVert \cdot \rVert_{\dot{B}^{s,s^{\prime}}_{p,r}}$ designates the  norm associated to $\dot{B}^{s,s^{\prime}}_{p,r}$.
<!-- end lem-4 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Applying  Lemma \ref{lem:maxi:regula}  to the parabolic equation \eqref{eq:W} 
yields: for all $t\in [0,T]$ and $j\in \mathbb{Z},$
\begin{align*}
    \left\Vert W_j(t)\right\Vert_{L^p}\le C\biggl(e^{-c_02^{2j }t} \left\Vert W_{0,j}\right\Vert_{L^p}+\int^t_0 e^{-c_02^{2 j}(t-\tau)} \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j g(\tau)\right\Vert_{L^p}d\tau \biggr),
\end{align*}
where $c_0$ and $C$ are positive constant and we have used the notation  $W_j=\Dot{\Delta}_j W$. Integrating the previous inequality with respect to time on $ [0,T] $ implies
\begin{align}
\label{est:p:W:1}
    \left\Vert W_j(t)\right\Vert_{L^p}+ 2^{2j}c\int^t_0  \left\Vert W_j\right\Vert_{L^p}\le C\biggl( \left\Vert W_{0,j}\right\Vert_{L^p}+\int^t_0 \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j g(\tau)\right\Vert_{L^p}d\tau \biggr),
\end{align}
where $c$ is a positive constant.
\smallbreak
In order to bound the right-hand side, we decompose $g$ into  $g=g^t+g^{V^2}+f^f+g^V+\Bar{g}^V$
as in \eqref{def:g}.
The first term can be bounded by means of \eqref{product_propo1} and of Proposition \ref{propo:comp}. We get
\begin{align*}
    \left\Vert {g^t} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\lesssim  \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\left\Vert \partial_t V\right\Vert_{L^\infty}+ \left\Vert V\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}.
\end{align*}
Using the embedding critical embedding
\begin{equation}\label{embed:crit}
\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}\hookrightarrow L^\infty,\end{equation} we arrive at
\begin{equation}    \label{est:gt}
    \left\Vert {g^t} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\lesssim  \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+  \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}.
    \end{equation}
To handle $ g^{V^2}$ we use \eqref{product_propo1} combined with Proposition \ref{propo:comp} and Bernstein inequality \eqref{bern:ine}. It holds
\begin{align*}
    \left\Vert {g^{V^2}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}&\lesssim \left\Vert {\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^d (\overline{Z}^{\alpha\beta}-Z^{\alpha\beta}(U))\partial_\beta V^2 } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\\
    &\lesssim \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\left\Vert \nabla V^2\right\Vert_{L^\infty}+ \left\Vert V\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\left\Vert {\nabla V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}},
\end{align*}
which becomes, using again \eqref{embed:crit}, 
\begin{equation}     \label{est:gV2}
     \left\Vert {g^{V^2}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\lesssim 
     \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}}.
    \end{equation}
Similarly taking into account the form of $f$ and \eqref{small:data:inf}, we have 
\begin{align}
    \label{est:gf}
    \begin{split}
     \left\Vert {g^{f}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}&\lesssim  \left\Vert {\Upsilon(U)\nabla U\otimes \nabla U} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\\
     &\lesssim  (1+\left\Vert V\right\Vert_{L^\infty})\left\Vert \nabla V\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}+ \left\Vert \nabla V\right\Vert_{L^\infty}^2\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}} )\\
     &\lesssim \left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}} +\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}^2\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}} .
      \end{split}
\end{align}

The term $g^V$ can be bounded directly using \eqref{product_propo1} and \eqref{embed:crit}. We have 
\begin{align}
    \label{est:gV}\nonumber
     \left\Vert {g^{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}} &\lesssim \left\Vert V\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}} + \left\Vert \nabla V\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}\\
      &\lesssim \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}} +  \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}}.
      \end{align}
As the operators  $\mathcal{S}_{12}(D)$ , $\mathcal{S}_{21}(D)$ and  $\mathcal{Z}(D)$ are homogeneous of degree $0$, applying Bernstein inequality \eqref{bern:ine:M} allows us to bound the last term $\Bar{g}^{V}$. It holds
\begin{align}
    \label{est:bar{g}V}
    \begin{split}
  \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j(\Bar{g}^{V})\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}\lesssim 2^{-j}  \left\Vert \partial_t V_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}.
      \end{split}
\end{align}
Inserting \eqref{est:gV}, \eqref{est:bar{g}V},  \eqref{est:gt} and \eqref{est:gV2} into \eqref{est:p:W:1}, then multiplying the obtained inequality by $2^{j\frac{d}{p_i}}$
and summing up on $j\ge N_0+2$ completes the proof of \eqref{2:est:p:W}.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lem-5: body_md -->
\label{est:V1:hf:lem}
Under assumptions (\textbf{H1})-(\textbf{H7}), It holds:
        
               
                 
               
             
                                                                                                                                                                                                                     
                                                                                                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                            
                                                                       
                                                                                                                                                                  
               
           
             
   
\begin{multline}\label{est:z:1}
\left\Vert {V^1} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})}^{h}+  \left\Vert {V^1} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})}^{h}\lesssim \left\Vert {V^1_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}^{h}+\int^T_0 \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\\
     + \int^T_0 \left(\left\Vert {{W}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}}^{h}+\left\Vert {{V^2}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}^{h}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}}  \right)\cdotp
     \end{multline}
<!-- end lem-5 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Throughout this proof, we will use repeatedly the fact that the matrix $B$ is a constant. 
Applying $\Dot{\Delta}_j$ to the equation \eqref{eq:V1=f(W)}  and denoting $ z_j= \Dot{\Delta}_j z$ leads to 
\begin{align}
    \label{eq:zj}
     \partial_t z_j+\sum_{\alpha=1}^d H^\alpha\partial_\alpha  z_j+ B(D) z_j=R_j+\Dot{\Delta}_j h
\end{align}
 with $h$ given by \eqref{def:ht:V:V2} and
 \begin{align}
     \label{def:Rj}
    R_j\overset{\rm def}{=}  -\sum_{\alpha=1}^d \left[\Dot{\Delta}_j,H^\alpha(U)- H^\alpha(\overline{U})\right]\partial_\alpha z.
 \end{align}
  Let us denote $(z^i_j)_{i}$, $(B(D)z_j)_i$ (with $i=1,\cdots,n_1$) respectively the components of the vector $z_j$ and $ B(D)z_j $ and $(H^\alpha_i)_i$ the diagonal components of the matrix $ H^\alpha$. \\
Multiplying \eqref{eq:zj} by the vector $(\overline{z}^i_j|z^i_j|^{p_i-2})_i$, using Leibniz formula on the convective term, taking the real part and remembering that the matrices $H^\alpha$ are diagonal yields for $i=1,2$
                                                                                                                                                                                                 
\begin{multline*}
     \frac{1}{p_i}\frac{d}{dt}|z_j|^{p_i}+\frac{1}{p_i}\sum_{\alpha=1}^d \sum_{k=1}^{n_1}\partial_\alpha\left(H^\alpha_k|z_j^k|^{p_i}_{}\right) -\frac{1}{p_i}\sum_{k=1}^{n_1}\sum_{\alpha=1}^d\partial_\alpha (H^\alpha_i(U))|z^k_j|^{p_i}+\sum_{k=1}^{n_1} (B(D)z_j)_k z_j^k |z_j^k|^{p_i-2}\\
     \le |z_j|^{p_i-1}(|R_j|+|\Dot{\Delta}_j h|).
 \end{multline*}
 Then, integrating on $\mathbb{R}^d$ and remembering that $B$ is a constant diagonal matrix with positive entries yields for some  positive constants $c_0$ and $C,$ 
                                                                                                                                                    
 \begin{multline}     \label{est:V1:ini}
         \frac{1}{p_i}\frac{d}{dt}\left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i}+ c_0\left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i} \le C\left\Vert \nabla H(U)\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i}+\left\Vert R_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}} \left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i-1}\\
     +C \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j h\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}} \left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i-1},
      \end{multline}

Combining the results of Proposition \ref{propo:comp} with the embedding \eqref{embed:crit} yields
\begin{align}
    \label{est: H}
    \left\Vert \nabla H(U)\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\lesssim \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}.
\end{align}
 Applying directly \eqref{comm:est:p} (with $\sigma=\frac{d}{p_i}+1$, $p=p_i$, $q=\widetilde{p}=p_2$)  and using the embeddings $ \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}\hookrightarrow L^\infty  \cap \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty} $ and \eqref{embed:crit} leads to
 \begin{align}     \label{est:Rj}
     \left\Vert R_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}} &\le Cc_j 2^{-j(\frac{d}{p_i}+1)}\left( \lVert \nabla V \rVert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}} \left\Vert {z} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}+ \left\Vert \nabla z\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\left\Vert {H(U)-H(\overline{U})} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\right)\nonumber\\
     &\le Cc_j 2^{-j(\frac{d}{p_i}+1)}\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}.
 \end{align}
Owing to the product property \eqref{product_propo1} and the embedding  $ \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}\hookrightarrow L^\infty$ we get the following bound on $h^{V^2}_1$: for all $j\ge N_{0}+2$
\begin{align}
    \label{est: h^v^2}
    \left\Vert {h^{V^2}_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}^h\lesssim \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_i,1}}.
\end{align}
 Since the homogeneous operators $P(\overline{S}^0_{11})^{-1}\overline{S}^\alpha_{12}\partial_\alpha$ and $ P (\overline{S}^0_{11})^{-1}  \mathcal{S}_{12}(D)\mathcal{Z}^{-1}(D)\mathcal{S}_{22}(D) $ are respectively of degree 1 and $0$,  we have from Bernstein inequality
 \begin{align}
   \label{est: h^W}
    \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j h^{W}\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}\lesssim   \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j \nabla W\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}\quad \text{and} \quad  \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j h^{V^2}_0\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}\lesssim \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j V^2\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}} .
 \end{align}

         
                
             
                    
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              
           



                                                                     
             
                  
             
                                                                                                     
                                                                                                                                                     
               
           
                                                          
                                                                                     
                                                                                                                                                      
             
                      
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          
           
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      
                
                      
                                                                                                                                                                                                     
                                                                                                                                                                                      
              
                                                                                          
              
    
Hence, plugging \eqref{est: H}, \eqref{est:Rj}, \eqref{est: h^v^2} and \eqref{est: h^W}  into \eqref{est:V1:ini} yields for all $j\in\mathbb Z,$
\begin{multline}
\label{est:V1:pi:0}
    \frac{1}{p_i}\frac{d}{dt}\left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i}+ \left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i} \lesssim \left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i}\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}+c_j2^{-j(\frac{d}{p_i}+1)}\left(\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\right.\\
       \left.   + \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}}+ \left\Vert {V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}^{h}+\left\Vert {W} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}}^{h} \right) \left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i-1}.
\end{multline}
        
               
             
                   
                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 
                                                                                               
               
           
             
   
In the sequel we will choose $N$ large enough  so that the last term in the previous inequality  be absorbed by $ \mathbb{V}_{p_i} $.\\ 
Finally, using lemma \ref{lem_der_int} (with $p=p_i$, $X=\left\Vert z_j\right\Vert_{L^{p_i}}^{p_i}$), then multiplying the obtained  inequality by $2^{j\frac{d}{p_i}+1}$, summing up $j\ge N_{0}+2$, gives us
\begin{multline*}
   \left\Vert {z} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})}^{h}+  \left\Vert {z} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1})}^{h}\lesssim \left\Vert {z(0)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}^{h}+\int^T_0 \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {z} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}
   +\int^T_0 \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}\\
     + \int^T_0 \left(\left\Vert {{W}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}}^{h}+\left\Vert {{V^2}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+1}_{p_i,1}}^{h}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}+2}_{p_i,1}}  \right)
     \cdotp\end{multline*}
Remembering that $V^1=P^{-1}z$ with $P$ a constant matrix (independent of $U$) completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- propo-2: body_md -->
\label{propo:est:lf:V}
    Let $d\ge 2$ and $p_2$ be such that 
    \begin{align}
        \label{cnd:p2:lf}
         p_2<2d \quad \text{and} \quad 2\le p_2\le \min\left(4,\frac{2d}{d-2}\right)\cdotp
    \end{align}
Consider a smooth (decreasing) solution of \eqref{eq:base:V} under assumptions
 (\textbf{H1})-(\textbf{H7}). Assume that the solution $V$ verifies the smallness condition \eqref{smaal:cond:V}. Then, the following inequality holds true:
\begin{equation}
    \label{est:lf:V}
     \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}_{2,1})}^{l}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}+1}_{2,1})}^{l}
    \lesssim \left\Vert {V_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}_{2,1}}^{l}+ \mathbb{V}_{p_2}^2(1+\mathbb{V}_{p_2}).
\end{equation}
<!-- end propo-2 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
We need to differentiate the following functional that contains enough information to get inequality \eqref{est:lf:V}:
   \begin{equation}
    \label{def:Ij}
   \forall j\le N_0+1 \quad \mathcal{L}_j\overset{def}{=} \frac12\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \overline{S}^0 V_j\cdot V_j+ 2^j \mathcal{I}_j\simeq  \left\Vert V_j\right\Vert_{L^2}^2 ,
\end{equation}
    with 
    \begin{equation}
    \label{df:corect}
    \mathcal{I}_j \overset{def}{=}  \sum_{k=1}^{n-1}  \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}\varepsilon_k {\rm  Re} (M_\omega N_\omega^{k-1} \widehat{V_j}\cdot M_\omega N_\omega^{k} \widehat{V_j} )
\end{equation}
and where $\varepsilon_1,\cdots,\varepsilon_{n-1}>0$ are chosen suitably small enough. The matrices $M_\omega$, $N_\omega$ have been defined in \eqref{A:def}, \eqref{B_ome:def} respectively.
\medbreak
We note that
\begin{equation}
\label{dt:lyp}
  \frac12  \frac{d}{dt} \mathcal{L}_j= \frac{d}{dt} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \overline{S}^0\partial_t V_j\cdot V_j+2^j\frac{d}{dt} \mathcal{I}_j.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{\textbf{First Step: Energy estimates for the first term of \eqref{dt:lyp}.}} 
We localize System \eqref{eq:lindiff} by means of the operator $\Dot{\Delta}_j$ as follows
\begin{align}
\label{eq:lh:V}
     \overline{S}^0\partial_t V_j+\sum_{\alpha=1}^d \overline{S}^\alpha\partial_\alpha V_j-\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^d\overline{Y}^{\alpha\beta} \partial_{\alpha\beta}V_j = \Dot{\Delta}_j F\quad \text{with} \quad V_j \overset{\rm def}{=} \Dot{\Delta}_j V. 
 \end{align}
For $j\le N_{0}+1 $, taking the scalar product in $L^2(\mathbb{R}^d;\mathbb{R}^n)$ with $V_j$, then performing an integration by parts in the second term and using the symmetry of the matrices $S^\alpha(U)$ yields,
\begin{align}
 \label{1':es:hf:}
 \begin{split}
    \frac12 \frac{d}{dt} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}  \overline{S}^0 V_j\cdot  V_j +\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^d\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \overline{Z}^{\alpha\beta} \partial_{\alpha\beta}V_j^2 \cdot V_j^2  &\le \left\Vert V_j\right\Vert_{L^2}\left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j F\right\Vert_{L^2}.
                                                                     
      \end{split}
 \end{align}

Next, thanks to \eqref{small:data:inf},  \eqref{strong_elli} and remarking that the smooth function $V_j$ is spectrally supported in dyadic
annuli of size $2^j$ leads to:
\begin{align}
 \label{1:es:hf:}
 \begin{split}
    \frac12 \frac{d}{dt} \int_{\mathbb{R}^d}  \overline{S}^0 V_j\cdot  V_j +\overline{c_1} 2^{2j} \left\Vert V^2_j\right\Vert_{L^2}^2 &\lesssim \left\Vert V_j\right\Vert_{L^2}\left\Vert  \Dot{\Delta}_j F\right\Vert_{L^2}
                                         
      \end{split}
 \end{align}
 where $c_1$ is the constant appearing in \eqref{strong_elli}.
\paragraph{\textbf{Second step: Differentiation of $ \mathcal{I}_j$.}} As we can see in \eqref{1:es:hf:}, we miss the dissipation of the component $V^1$. To recover the dissipation of all the components of $V$, we have to look at the time derivative of $\mathcal{I}_j$ defined in \eqref{df:corect}. Applying  Lemma \ref{lem:derI} and using the fact that the support of $\widehat{V}_j$ is contained in an annulus of magnitude $2^j$ (this implies in particular that the term $\rho$ appearing in Inequality \eqref{lya:lo:in} is equivalent to $2^j$, that is, $\rho \simeq 2^j$ ) we get 
\begin{equation}
\label{low:lya:eqj}
    \frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{I}_j +2^{j} \sum_{k=1}^{n-1}  \varepsilon_k |M_\omega N_\omega^{k} \widehat{V}_j|^2 \le C\varepsilon_0\max(2^{j},2^{3j})| M_\omega  \widehat{V}_j|^2+C \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j F\right\Vert_{L^2}\left\Vert V_j\right\Vert_{L^2}.
\end{equation}

\paragraph{\textbf{Third step: Estimating $\mathcal{L}_j$}}
Multiplying inequality \eqref{low:lya:eqj} by $2^j$ and putting it together with \eqref{1:es:hf:} provides a positive constant $c>0$ such that for all $j\le N_{0}+1$
\begin{align*}
                       
    \frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_j+  2^{2j}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1} \varepsilon_k\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} |M_\omega N_\omega^{k}\widehat{V}_j|^2 +  c2^{2j} \left\Vert V^2_j\right\Vert_{L^2}^2\lesssim   
                                                        
    \left\Vert V_j\right\Vert_{L^2}\left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j F\right\Vert_{L^2}+ \varepsilon_0 2^{2j}\int_{\mathbb{R}^d}| M_\omega  \widehat{V}_j|^2.
\end{align*}
  Recall that the matrix $M_\omega$ has the diagonal form \eqref{B_ome:def}. Choosing $\varepsilon_0$ small enough, the last term of the right-hand side term can be absorbed by the left-hand side.                          
 For all $j\le N_{0}+1$, it therefore holds (up to a slight modification of $\varepsilon_0$)
that 

 \begin{align}
    \label{01:est:ly:l}
    \frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_j+  2^{2j}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1} \varepsilon_k\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} |M_\omega N_\omega^{k}\widehat{V}_j|^2
                                                      
    \lesssim  
    & \left\Vert V_j\right\Vert_{L^2}\left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j F\right\Vert_{L^2}.
\end{align}

\paragraph{\textbf{Fourth step: Nonlinear estimates}}
In order to bound the nonlinear term $F,$ we will use Lemma \ref{lem:produ:lf:hf}, that is, the substitute of Proposition \ref{propo_produc_BH} since the high frequencies and low frequencies of $V$ belong to Besov spaces with different Lebesgue indices. 
Here, $p_2<2d$ and $2\le p_2\le \min\left(4,\frac{2d}{d-2}\right)$ will be required.
 
Recall that $F=F^V+F^t+F^{V^2}+f$  where $F^V, F^t$ and $F^{V^2}$ have been defined in \eqref{F_t} while $f$ is defined in \eqref{globa:f=f(f1,f2)}.
Now,   taking $s=\frac{d}{p_2}-1$, $p=p_2$ and $\sigma=1$ in \eqref{lem:pro:lf:hf} yields
\begin{multline*}
     \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j F^V\right\Vert_{L^2}\lesssim 2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}c_j
        \sum_{\alpha=1}^d\biggl( \left\Vert {\Bar{S}^\alpha-S^\alpha(U)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}  \left\Vert {\partial_\alpha V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\\+ \left\Vert {\Bar{S}^\alpha-S^\alpha(U)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}  \left\Vert {\partial_\alpha V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\biggr)\cdotp
\end{multline*}
Bearing in mind \eqref{smaal:cond:V}, to control the first term (resp. the second term) in the r.h.s.,  we leverage \eqref{comp:u:propo:p} (resp. \eqref{comp:uv:propo:1}) and we get:
\begin{align}
     \label{est:Fv:til:V}
     \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j F^V\right\Vert_{L^2}
       \lesssim 2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)} c_j\biggl(\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\biggr).
\end{align}

Similarly, we obtain  
\begin{align}
    \label{est:Ft}\nonumber
        \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j F^t\right\Vert_{L^2}&\lesssim 2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}c_j \biggl(\left\Vert {(\overline{S}^0-S^0(U))} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {(\overline{S}^0-S^0(U))} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\biggr)\\\nonumber
        &\lesssim 2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}c_j\biggl( \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\biggr)\\\nonumber
        &\lesssim 2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}  c_j
        \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\biggl( \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1}}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}} \biggr) \\
        &\hspace{1cm}+2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}  c_j
        \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\biggl(\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+2}_{p_2,1}}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1}}\biggr),
\end{align}
where in the last inequality  we used Inequality \eqref{est:ptV:w:v} both in the two terms, with $\kappa=\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1$, $q=p_2$  and $\kappa=\frac{d}{{p_2}}$, $q=p_2$ respectively.


Next, combining  \eqref{lem:pro:lf:hf} (with $s=\frac{d}{p_2}-1$, $p=p_2$ and $\sigma=1$), \eqref{comp:uv:propo:1} and \eqref{comp:u:propo:p} and the smallness condition \eqref{smaal:cond:V}, we obtain  
$$\left\Vert {f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}_{2,1}} \lesssim \left\Vert {\nabla V\otimes \nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{2}-1}_{2,1}}
+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V\otimes \nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}+
\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V\otimes \nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}$$
whence, using once more the same inequalities,
               
                                                                                                                                                                                                                            
             
                                                                                                                     
\begin{align}
    \label{est:f:lf}
    \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j f\right\Vert_{L^2}\lesssim  2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}c_j\biggl(
(1+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}})\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}
    + \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}^2\biggr)\cdotp
\end{align}
 Estimating  the last term $ F^{V^2}$ is based on the following decomposition:
\begin{equation}
    \label{dec:F:V2}
    F^{V^2}=\sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^d \partial_\alpha (r^{\alpha\beta}(U))\,\partial_\beta V+ \sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^d r^{\alpha\beta}(U)\partial^2_{\alpha\beta} V ,
\end{equation}
where $r^{\alpha\beta}(U)$ is the function defined in \eqref{F_t}. In particular, we have $ r^{\alpha\beta}(\overline{U})=0$
As $p_2<2d$ and $2\le p_2\le \min\left(4,\frac{2d}{d-2}\right)$, applying again \eqref{lem:pro:lf:hf} with $s=\frac{d}{p_2}-1$, $p=p_2$ and $\sigma=1$, using Proposition \ref{propo:comp} and observing that the matrices $r^{\alpha\beta}$ don't have any effect on the component  $V^1$   provides
\begin{align*}
\left\Vert  (\partial_\alpha r^{\alpha\beta}(U))\,\partial_\beta V\right\Vert_{L^2}&\lesssim 2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)} c_j \biggl( \left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}} \biggr),\\
 \left\Vert   r^{\alpha\beta}(U)\partial^2_{\alpha\beta} V\right\Vert_{L^2} &\lesssim 2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)} c_j \biggl(  \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla^2 V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}} + \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}} \biggr),\end{align*}
whence
\begin{equation}\label{est:FV2}
        \left\Vert \Dot{\Delta}_j F^{V^2}\right\Vert_{L^2}\lesssim 2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)} c_j \biggl(\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla^2 V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}} \biggr)\cdotp
\end{equation}  

\paragraph{\textbf{Last step : Final estimate of $\mathcal{L}_j$}}
Inserting \eqref{est:Fv:til:V}, \eqref{est:Ft},  \eqref{est:f:lf}  and 
\eqref{est:FV2} into \eqref{01:est:ly:l}  and observing that 
$$\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}^2\lesssim  
\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}$$
yields
 \begin{multline}\label{2:est:ly:l}
    \frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_j+  2^{2j}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^d} \varepsilon_k |B_\omega A_\omega^{k}\widehat{V}_j|^2 \lesssim    
     2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}c_j \left\Vert V_j\right\Vert_{L^2}  \biggl((1+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}})\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}^2\\
    + (\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}})  \left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}
    + \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+2}_{p_2,1}}
    \biggr)\cdotp
       \end{multline}

       Thanks to  Shizuta-Kawashima (SK) condition (see Lemma \ref{lem:SK_diss}) and the fact that
\begin{align}
    \label{eq:Lj:Vj}
    \mathcal{L}_j\simeq \left\Vert V_j\right\Vert_{L^2}^2
\end{align}
we thus get some $\bar c_0$ such that for all $j\le N_{0}+1$
\begin{multline}
    \label{3:est:ly:l}
\frac{d}{dt}\mathcal{L}_j+  2^{2j}\bar c_0\mathcal{L}_j \lesssim  2^{-j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}c_j \sqrt{\mathcal{L}_j }  \biggl( 
(1+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}})\left\Vert {\nabla V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}^2\\
    + (\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}})  \left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}
    + \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+2}_{p_2,1}}\biggr)\cdotp
\end{multline}
Therefore taking $p=2$ and $X= \mathcal{L}_j$ in Lemma  \eqref{lem_der_int}
 then multiplying by $2^{j(\frac{d}{2}-1)} $  delivers for all $j\le N_{0}+1$ and $t\in [0,T]$
\begin{multline}
    \label{4:est:ly:l}
    2^{j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_j(t)}+  2^{j(\frac{d}{2}+1)}\bar c_0\int^t_0\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_j} \lesssim 2^{j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_j(0)} \\
    + \int^t_0 c_j\biggl((1+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}})\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}^2
    + (\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}})  \left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}
    + \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+2}_{p_2,1}}\biggr)\cdotp\end{multline}
 

Let us now simplify the right-hand side of the previous inequality. Thanks to \eqref{est:V:Bs:p:Vi} and \eqref{pr:int:V:V:} we have
\begin{align*}
    \int^t_0 (\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}) \left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}
    &\le (\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1})}+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})})\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}\\
    &\lesssim \mathbb{V}_{p_2}^2,\end{align*}
    \begin{equation*}
    \int^t_0 \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+2}_{p_2,1}}\lesssim \left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {V^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+2}_{p_2,1})}\lesssim  \mathbb{V}_{p_2}^2
\end{equation*}
and 
\begin{gather*}
    \int^t_0  (1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}})     \left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}^2 \lesssim (1+\left\Vert {V} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1})}) \int^t_0 \left\Vert { V} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}^2\lesssim (1+\mathbb{V}_{p_2})\mathbb{V}_{p_2}^2\cdotp 
\end{gather*}
 Inequality \eqref{2:est:ly:l} becomes

 
\begin{equation}
    \label{5:est:ly:l}
    2^{j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_j(t)}+  2^{j(\frac{d}{2}+1)}\bar c_0\int^t_0\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_j} \lesssim 2^{j(\frac{d}{2}-1)}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}_j(0)} +c_j (1+ \mathbb{V}_{p_2})\mathbb{V}_{p_2}^2.
\end{equation}
       Summing over $j\le N_{0}+1$ and remembering \eqref{eq:Lj:Vj} yields \eqref{est:lf:V}.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lem-6: body_md -->
\label{est:uni}
 Assume that $2\le p_2<2d$. Let $V_1$ and $V_1$ be two solutions of \eqref{eq:base:V} under assumptions (\textbf{H1})-(\textbf{H7})  on $[0,T]$ supplemented by initial data $V_{1,0}$ and $V_{2,0},$ respectively. Then, there exists a constant  $C>0$ depending on $ \left\Vert {(V_2,V_1)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})} $such that $ \widetilde{V}\overset{\rm def}{=} V_1-V_2  $ satisfies the following a priori estimate 
  \begin{multline}
      \label{est:lem:tV}
            \Vert\widetilde{V}\Vert_{\mathbb{F}(T)} \le   C\biggl(\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^1_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+  \left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}\\
    + \sqrt{T}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert { (V_1,V_2)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}+ \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\nabla V_1^2} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\biggr)\cdotp
        \end{multline}
<!-- end lem-6 -->
<!-- rmq-6: body_md -->
Let us emphasize that the stability inequality \eqref{est:lem:tV} is derived without relying on any smallness condition.
<!-- end rmq-6 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Throughout the proof, we agree that $A\lesssim B$ means that there exists a positive constant $C$ depending only on $p,d$, the matrices involved in system \eqref{systemE} and $  \left\Vert {(V_2,V_1)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})} $ such that $A\le CB$.  We divide the proof of Lemma \ref{est:uni} into several steps. Let us start with the following estimate for $\partial_t \widetilde{V}$.
\paragraph{\textbf{Step 1: Estimates for $\partial_t \widetilde{V}$.}}
\begin{lem}
    \label{lem:pt:tV:k}
 For all  $p_2\in [1,2d[ $ and $\kappa\in [\frac{d}{q}-1,\frac{d}{q}]$, we have for some 
 constant $C$ depending only on $d,$ $p_2,$ the parameters of the system and $  \left\Vert {(V_2,V_1)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})} $ :                                                                           
\begin{multline}
    \label{pt:tV:k}
    \left\Vert { \partial_t \widetilde{V} } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{p_2,1}} \leq C\biggl( \left\Vert {\nabla \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{p_2,1}}\\
     + \left\Vert { \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+2}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}} 
    + \left\Vert { \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}  \left\Vert { V^2_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+2}_{p_2,1}}+  \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\\
     +  \left\Vert { \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\bigl(  \left\Vert { V_2^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+2}_{p_2,1}}   +\left\Vert {V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\bigr)\biggr)\cdotp
    \end{multline}
   \end{lem}
\begin{proof}
From \eqref{sysdif::modif},  $\partial_t \widetilde{V}$ satisfies
   \begin{align*}
       \partial_t \widetilde{V} &=-\sum_{\alpha}{(S^0(U_1))^{-1}S^\alpha (U_1) \partial_\alpha \widetilde{V}}- \sum_{\alpha}{((S^0(U_1))^{-1}S^\alpha (U_1)-(S^0(U_2))^{-1}S^\alpha (U_2)) \partial_\alpha V_2}  \\
       &+\sum_{\alpha,\beta}{ (S^0(U_1))^{-1}\partial_\alpha (Y(U_1)^{\alpha\beta} \partial_\beta \widetilde{V})  }+ \sum_{\alpha,\beta}{ (S^0(U_2))^{-1}\partial_\alpha \left((Y(U_1)^{\alpha\beta}-Y(U_2)^{\alpha\beta}) \partial_\beta V_2\right)  }\\
       &+ \sum_{\alpha,\beta}{ \left( (S^0(U_1))^{-1}-  (S^0(U_2))^{-1} \right)\partial_\alpha (Y(U_1)^{\alpha\beta} \partial_\beta V_2)  }
.
   \end{align*}
 As $1\le p_2<2d$ and $\kappa\in [\frac{d}{p_2}-1,\frac{d}{p_2}]$, taking advantage of  Proposition \ref{propo_produc_BH}  yields \eqref{pt:tV:k}.
                   
                                                                                                                                                   
                                                                                                              
                                                                                                                                             
                                                                                                                                                                                      
                 
 \end{proof}

\paragraph{\textbf{Step 2: Estimates for $ \widetilde{V}^1$.}}
Instead of $\widetilde{V}^1$, we shall obtain estimates for $$\widetilde{z}\overset{\rm def}{=} z_1-z_2= P(V_1^1-V^1_2)=P\widetilde{V}^1.$$ In fact, since $P$ is an invertible and constant matrix, any Besov norm of $\widetilde{V}^1$ and $\widetilde{z}$ are equivalent.
\smallbreak
Remark that, from \eqref{eq:V1=f(H)},$ \widetilde{z}  $  is solution of:
\begin{align}
    \label{eq:tz}
     &\partial_t  \widetilde{z}  + \sum_{\alpha=1}^d H^\alpha(U_1)\partial_\alpha \widetilde{z}=\widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_1)-\widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_2)- \sum_{\alpha=1}^d \left( H^\alpha(U_2)-H^\alpha(U_1)\right)\partial_\alpha z_2
\end{align}
with 
\begin{align*}
    \widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U)\overset{\rm def}{=} - \sum_{\alpha=1}^d P ( S^0_{11}(U))^{-1} S^\alpha_{12}(U)\partial_\alpha V^2.
\end{align*}

    Following the proof of Lemma \ref{est:V1:hf:lem}, we deduce the following estimate 
    \begin{multline}
    \label{est:tv1=f(source:terms)}
         \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^1} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1})}\lesssim \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^1_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}+\int^T_0 \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\\
        + \int^T_0 \biggl( \left\Vert {\widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_1)-\widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_2)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_1}}}_{p_1,1}}+ \left\Vert { \left( H^\alpha(U_2)-H^\alpha(U_1)\right) \partial_\alpha z_2 } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_1}}}_{p_1,1}}\biggr)\cdotp
    \end{multline}
       
   In order to estimate the right-hand side, we have to use many times the fact that the product is an algebra on the space $\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_i}}_{p_i,1}$. Taking advantage of Proposition \ref{propo:comp}, we get
   \begin{align*}
       \int^T_0 \left\Vert { \left( H^\alpha(U_2)-H^\alpha(U_1)\right)\partial_\alpha  z_2 } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}\lesssim   \sqrt{T}\left\Vert { \widetilde{V} } \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert { \nabla V^1_2 } \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}.
   \end{align*}
Using the decomposition 
\begin{multline}
\label{decomp:tidle:h:V2}
    \widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_1)-\widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_2)= \sum_{\alpha=1}^d P(S^0_{11}(\overline{U}))^{-1}S^\alpha_{12}(\overline{U})\partial_\alpha  \widetilde{V}^2  \\
      -\sum_{\alpha=1}^d P(S^0_{11}(\overline{U}))^{-1}S^\alpha_{12}(\overline{U}) -(S^0_{11}(U_2))^{-1}S^\alpha_{12}(U_{2})) \partial_\alpha  \widetilde{V}^2\\
      +\sum_{\alpha=1}^d P\left( (S^0_{11}(U_1))^{-1}S^\alpha_{12}(U_1)- (S^0_{11}(U_2))^{-1}S^\alpha_{12}(U_{2})\right)\partial_\alpha V^2_1,
\end{multline}
combined with Proposition \ref{propo:comp}, we obtain
\begin{multline*}
     \int^T_0 \left\Vert {  \widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_1)-\widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_2) } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}^h\\\lesssim 
      \left\Vert {\nabla \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}+ \left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}+ \sqrt{T}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {V^2_1} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}.
\end{multline*}
Gathering the above estimates and using once more the embedding $ \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1} \hookrightarrow L^\infty $, we obtain for large enough $K>0$
    \begin{multline}
\label{est:tz:}
    \frac{1}{K( 1+\left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}) }\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^1} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1})}  \le \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^1_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\\
    +\sqrt{T} \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {(V_2, V_1)} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1})}
     +\frac{1}{2}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})} .
    \end{multline}
\paragraph{\textbf{Step 3: Estimates for $ \widetilde{V}^2$ .}}
From \eqref{rel:W:V2}, we discover that $ \widetilde{V}^2 $ is solution of 

\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:tW}
    \overline{S}^0_{22}\partial_t \widetilde{V}^2 -\Delta\mathcal{Z}(D) \widetilde{V}^2= (g^t(U_1)-g^t(U_2))+ (g^V(U_1)-g^V(U_2))+(g^{V^2}(U_1)-g^{V^2}(U_2)).
\end{equation}
   Following the lines of the proof of the Lemma \ref{est:M:lem} we arrive at
   \begin{multline*}
    \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1})} +  \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_i,1})}\lesssim \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_i,1}} \\
    + \int^T_0  \left\Vert { (g^t(U_1)-g^t(U_2), g^V(U_1)-g^V(U_2),g^{V^2}(U_1)-g^{V^2}(U_2))} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}}.
   \end{multline*}
   In the spirit of calculations leading to \eqref{est:tz:}, we are going to estimate the non linear terms on the right-hand side of the previous inequality. The general idea is similar to what we did for the term $ \widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_1)-\widetilde{h}^{V^2}(U_2) $ in \eqref{est:tv1=f(source:terms)}. In order to bound the nonlinear terms, we have to decompose  each term of right-hand side of the above inequality (that is, $ g^t(U_1)-g^t(U_2), g^V(U_1)-g^V(U_2)$ and $g^{V^2}(U_1)-g^{V^2}(U_2)$) as follows:

   \begin{multline}
    \label{decomp:gt:V:V2}
       g^t(U_1)-g^t(U_2)=  (S^0_{22}(U_2)- S^0_{22}(U_1))\partial_t V_1^2+  (\overline{S}^0_{22}-S^0_{22}(U_2))\partial_t \widetilde{V}^2,\\
        g^V(U_1)-g^V(U_2)= \sum_{\alpha=1}^d\left((S^\alpha_{21}(U_2)-S^\alpha_{21}(U_1))\partial_\alpha V^1_1+(S^\alpha_{22}(U_2)-S^\alpha_{22}(U_1))\partial_\alpha V^2_1)\right)\\
        + \sum_{\alpha=1}^d\left((\overline{S}_{21}-S^\alpha_{21}(U_2))\partial_\alpha \widetilde{V}^1+(\overline{S}_{22}-S^\alpha_{22}(U_2)\partial_\alpha \widetilde{V}^2)\right),\\
        g^{V^2}(U_1)-g^{V^2}(U_2)= \sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^d \partial_\alpha\left((Z^{\alpha\beta}(U_2)-Z^{\alpha\beta}(U_1))\partial_\beta V^2_1\right)\\
        + \sum_{\alpha,\beta=1}^d \partial_\alpha\left((\overline{Z}^{\alpha\beta}-Z^{\alpha\beta}(U_2))\partial_\beta \widetilde{V}^2\right).
   \end{multline}
Now, using Proposition \ref{propo:comp} and the fact that
the product maps $\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}\times \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1}\mapsto \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1} $ (let us remember that $p_2<2d$), we easily get
\begin{multline}
\label{est:tg:t:V}
    \int^T_0 \left\Vert {g^t(U_1)-g^t(U_2)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}}\lesssim \int^T_0\left( \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V^2_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}}\right),\\
    \int^T_0 \left\Vert {g^V(U_1)-g^V(U_2)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}}\lesssim \sqrt{T}\left( \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\nabla V_1} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1})}\right.\\
    \left.+ \left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\nabla \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1})}\right),
\end{multline}
while using the product law  $\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}\times \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}\mapsto \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1} $  one may handle the term $g^{V^2}(U_1)-g^{V^2}(U_2) $ as follows 
\begin{multline}
    \label{est:tg:t:V2}
      \int^T_0 \left\Vert {g^{V^2}(U_1)-g^{V^2}(U_2)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}}\lesssim \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\nabla V_1^2} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\\+ \left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\nabla \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}.
\end{multline}
   
Let us now give more information about the first inequality of \eqref{est:tg:t:V}. Taking advantage of Lemma \ref{lem:pt:tV:k} (we take $\kappa=\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1$) and remembering that $p_2<2d$, one has
\begin{multline}\label{eq:dttildeV}
    \left\Vert {\partial_t \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}} \lesssim \left\Vert { \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}\bigl(1+\left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}+(1+\left\Vert {V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}})\left\Vert {V_2^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1}}\bigr)\\ + 
    \left\Vert { \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1}}(1+\left\Vert {V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}\bigr)\cdotp
\end{multline}
 Then one may write
\begin{multline}
    \label{1:est:tg:t:}
    \int^T_0 \left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}}\le \sqrt{T}C\left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\\
  +C \left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}
   + C\left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {V^2_2} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})},
\end{multline}
where $C$ depends on $  \left\Vert {(V_2,V_1)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})} $.\\
Meanwhile taking $\kappa=\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1$ in \eqref{pt:V:est} leads to
\begin{align*}
     \left\Vert {\partial_t V^2_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}} &\le C \left\Vert { V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert { V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1}},\;\; C=C(\left\Vert {V_1} \right\Vert_{L^{)}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\cdotp
\end{align*}
                                                                           
One may deduce that
\begin{multline}
    \label{2:est:tg:t:}
  \int^T_0 \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\partial_t V^2_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-1}_{p_2,1}}\le C \sqrt{T} \left\Vert {V_1} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\\
  + C\left\Vert {V^2_1} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}.
\end{multline}

Finally, combining \eqref{2:est:tg:t:}, \eqref{1:est:tg:t:}, \eqref{est:tg:t:V2} and  \eqref{est:tg:t:V}, we end with 
    \begin{multline}
\label{est:tW:}
\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1})}+  \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_i,1})}\le   \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_i,1}}\\
     +C\sqrt{T}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert { (V_1,V_2)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}+C  \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\nabla V_1^2} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\\
  + C\left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}.
    \end{multline}
with  $C>0$ depending on $ \left\Vert {(V_2,V_1)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})} $.
\paragraph{\textbf{Step 3: Proof of Lemma \ref{est:uni}}.}
Summing \eqref{est:tW:}, \eqref{est:tz:} together and remarking that the last term of \eqref{est:tz:} may be absorbed by the left-hand side of \eqref{est:tW:}, we get for all $t\ge 0$,
\begin{multline*}
     \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_2,1})}+ \frac{1}{2} \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_i,1})}+\frac{1}{ 2(1+\left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^1} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1})}\\
     \le  C\left(\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}-1}_{p_i,1}}+ \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^1_0} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\right)+  C\left\Vert {V_2} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{L^{1}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}+1}_{p_2,1})}\\
     C \sqrt{T}\left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert { (V_1,V_2,)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}+ C \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})}\left\Vert {\nabla V_1^2} \right\Vert_{L^{2}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})},
\end{multline*}
where $C>0$ depends on $  \left\Vert {(V_2,V_1)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})} $. By interpolation inequality in Besov spaces, one obtains the desired estimate of Lemma \ref{est:uni}.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lem-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:pt:tV:k}
 For all  $p_2\in [1,2d[ $ and $\kappa\in [\frac{d}{q}-1,\frac{d}{q}]$, we have for some 
 constant $C$ depending only on $d,$ $p_2,$ the parameters of the system and $  \left\Vert {(V_2,V_1)} \right\Vert_{L^{\infty}_T(\Dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}}_{p_2,1})} $ :                                                                           
\begin{multline}
    \label{pt:tV:k}
    \left\Vert { \partial_t \widetilde{V} } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{p_2,1}} \leq C\biggl( \left\Vert {\nabla \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert {\nabla V_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa}_{p_2,1}}\\
     + \left\Vert { \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+2}_{p_2,1}}+ \left\Vert {V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { \widetilde{V}^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}} 
    + \left\Vert { \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}  \left\Vert { V^2_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+2}_{p_2,1}}+  \left\Vert {\widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\\
     +  \left\Vert { \widetilde{V}} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,1}}\bigl(  \left\Vert { V_2^2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+2}_{p_2,1}}   +\left\Vert {V_1} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\kappa+1}_{p_2,1}}\left\Vert { V^2_2} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}+1}_{p_2,1}}\bigr)\biggr)\cdotp
    \end{multline}
<!-- end lem-7 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
From \eqref{sysdif::modif},  $\partial_t \widetilde{V}$ satisfies
   \begin{align*}
       \partial_t \widetilde{V} &=-\sum_{\alpha}{(S^0(U_1))^{-1}S^\alpha (U_1) \partial_\alpha \widetilde{V}}- \sum_{\alpha}{((S^0(U_1))^{-1}S^\alpha (U_1)-(S^0(U_2))^{-1}S^\alpha (U_2)) \partial_\alpha V_2}  \\
       &+\sum_{\alpha,\beta}{ (S^0(U_1))^{-1}\partial_\alpha (Y(U_1)^{\alpha\beta} \partial_\beta \widetilde{V})  }+ \sum_{\alpha,\beta}{ (S^0(U_2))^{-1}\partial_\alpha \left((Y(U_1)^{\alpha\beta}-Y(U_2)^{\alpha\beta}) \partial_\beta V_2\right)  }\\
       &+ \sum_{\alpha,\beta}{ \left( (S^0(U_1))^{-1}-  (S^0(U_2))^{-1} \right)\partial_\alpha (Y(U_1)^{\alpha\beta} \partial_\beta V_2)  }
.
   \end{align*}
 As $1\le p_2<2d$ and $\kappa\in [\frac{d}{p_2}-1,\frac{d}{p_2}]$, taking advantage of  Proposition \ref{propo_produc_BH}  yields \eqref{pt:tV:k}.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lem-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:prod}
Let $p_2\ge 2,\; 1\le r\le \infty$ and $\theta>-\frac{d}{p_2}>0$. We consider $f,g:\mathbb{R}^d\mapsto \mathbb{R}$ some functions. Then, there exists   $C>0$ such that
\begin{align}
    \label{prod:p2:gen}
    \left\Vert {gf } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C\left( (\left\Vert f\right\Vert_{L^\infty}+\left\Vert {f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty}})\left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}+ \left\Vert {f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}} \left\Vert g\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\right)\cdotp
\end{align}
In particular,  
\begin{align}
    \label{prod:p2:gen:s}
    \left\Vert {gf } \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C\bigl(\left\Vert f\right\Vert_{L^\infty}+\left\Vert {f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty}}\bigr)\left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}} \quad \text{if} \quad \theta<\frac{d}{p_2}\cdotp
\end{align}
Moreover, for any functions $u:\mathbb{R}^d\mapsto \mathbb{R}$ and $f_2:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$
with $f_2(0)=0,$ there exists a constant $C=C(f_2,\left\Vert u\right\Vert_{L^\infty},\theta)$ such that
\begin{align}
    \label{prod:2:p2}
    \left\Vert {gf_2(u)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C\left( (\left\Vert u\right\Vert_{L^\infty}+\left\Vert {u} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty}})\left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}+ \left\Vert {u} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}} \left\Vert g\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\right),\text{  if  } \theta>0
\end{align}
and 
\begin{align}
    \label{prod:2:p2:0}
    \left\Vert {gf_2(u)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C\bigl( \left\Vert u\right\Vert_{L^\infty}+\left\Vert {u} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty}}\bigr) \left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}} \quad \text{if } \quad\theta<\frac{d}{p_2}\cdotp
\end{align}
Finally, if $\theta<0$, then there still exists a constant $C=C(f_2,\left\Vert u\right\Vert_{L^\infty},\theta)>0$ such that  the following inequality holds
\begin{align}
    \label{prod:2':p2}
    \left\Vert {gf_2(u)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C\left( (\left\Vert u\right\Vert_{L^\infty}+\left\Vert {u} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty}})\left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}+ \left\Vert {u} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta+\sigma}_{2,r}} \left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{{p_2}}-\sigma}_{p_2,\infty}}\right)
\end{align}
for any $\sigma>0$ such that $\theta+\sigma>0$.
<!-- end lem-8 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
We recall the Bony decomposition
\[ f_1g=\Dot{T}_{f_1} g+\Dot{T}_g f_1+\Dot{R}(f_1,g).\]
    From the continuity of the paraproduct 
                                                                          
    we get
    \begin{align}
    \label{eq:Tf:g:Tg:f:s}
    \left\Vert {\Dot{T}_g f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C\left\Vert g\right\Vert_{L^\infty} \left\Vert {f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\quad \text{and} \quad 
         \left\Vert {\Dot{T}_{f} g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C\left\Vert f\right\Vert_{L^\infty}\left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}. 
    \end{align}
    As $\theta+\frac{d}{p_2}>0$ and $p_2\ge 2$ we easily obtain (with $\frac{1}{\lambda}\overset{\rm def}{=} \frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p_2}$)
    \begin{align}
    \label{R:f:g:s:est}
        \left\Vert {\Dot{R}(g, f)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C \left\Vert {\Dot{R}(g, f)} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta+\frac{d}{p_2}}_{\lambda,r}} \le C \left\Vert {f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty}} \left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}.
    \end{align}
   Putting together all these estimates gives \eqref{prod:p2:gen}. In order to prove \eqref{prod:p2:gen:s}, we have to bound $ \Dot{T}_g f$ differently. In fact, 
 in the case $ \theta<\frac{d}{p_2}$,  using the continuity of the paraproduct  and Besov embedding  \[  \Dot{T}:\dot{B}^{\theta-\frac{d}{p_2}}_{\Bar{p},r}\times \dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty} \longrightarrow \dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r} \quad \text{and} \quad \dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r} \hookrightarrow  \dot{B}^{\theta-\frac{d}{p_2}}_{\Bar{p},r} \]
    with $\frac{1}{2}=\frac{1}{p_2}+\frac{1}{\Bar{p}}$ we have 
    \begin{align*}
      \left\Vert {\Dot{T}_g f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\le C  \left\Vert {g} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\theta}_{2,r}}\left\Vert {f} \right\Vert_{\dot{B}^{\frac{d}{p_2}}_{p_2,\infty}}.
    \end{align*}
    Let us now prove \eqref{prod:2:p2}, \eqref{prod:2':p2} and \eqref{prod:2:p2:0}.\\  On the one hand, combining \eqref{prod:p2:gen} and Proposition \ref{propo:comp} leads to  \eqref{prod:2:p2}.\\
    On the other hand, if $\theta< \frac{d}{p_2}$, using \eqref{prod:p2:gen:s} and Proposition \ref{propo:comp} leads to \eqref{prod:2:p2:0}.\\                                                   
     Finally, if $\theta+\sigma>0$, combining \eqref{R:f:g:s:est}, the second inequality of \eqref{eq:Tf:g:Tg:f:s} together with the Proposition \ref{propo:comp} and the fact 
    \begin{align*}
